ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u5	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Government's draft law amending the Law on Scientific Degrees and Titles and on Degrees and Titles in Art and some other laws, print No 1287. The project deals with the regulation of the academic career path based on conducting research aimed at applying their results in the economic or social sphere. The current level of cooperation between science and business sectors in Poland is unsatisfactory. The share of R &amp; D expenditure in GDP is among the lowest in Europe. In analysing the cause of this situation, one of the factors limiting the scope of cooperation between the scientific community and the socio-economic environment is the lack of clearly defined opportunities for the development of academic careers on the basis of achievements in the implementation work. At the moment, there are no clear principles for the development of scientific careers based on implementation and development work in the socio-economic environment. This also reduces the interest of the scientific community in implementation work. The key solution contained in the project is the establishment of an implementation doctorate providing incentives to stimulate cooperation between the scientific community and the socio-economic environment. It should be noted that implementation PhDs have been successfully implemented in other Member States of the European Union for many years, including. in Denmark, France, Germany and recently also at European Union level. The new career path designed in the Act will enable you to gain a doctoral degree based on the achievements achieved in the implementation work. It is assumed that such career opportunities will provide incentives for those planning an academic career to focus it on the implementation of research results. The establishment of the implementation doctorate will provide additional incentives for young researchers to develop their careers on the basis of implementation activities. Increased interest in implementation works will translate into increased cooperation between scientific units and entities operating in their socio-economic environment, and this in turn will result in an increase in the innovation of the Polish economy and an improvement in the quality of life of society. The programme provides for the funding of costs related to doctoral studies in which training takes place in cooperation with the socio-economic environment, including the entrepreneur or other entity concerned by the specific area of research. The introduction of this needed and long-awaited solution in Poland, firstly, will contribute to the development of contacts between the scientific unit and the socio-economic environment, secondly, will help to intensify research with commercial potential and, thirdly, will enable the future scientist to gain experience in the field of research and development. In view of the importance and importance of the project, it has been subjected to a thorough work of the Emergency Sub-Committee, giving it the opportunity to have an additional meeting with the higher education community, the rectors and trade union representatives and to take into account further votes and comments, which is a domain of the work of Law and Justice. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: Bravo!]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u10	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz Club15 in relation to the government's draft law amending the Law on Scientific Degrees and Science title, contained in print no. 1287. In the original government project, major changes were made during the work of the subcommittee and the Committee on Education, Science and Youth. These changes refer to the procedure of habilitation, speaking in simplification, implementation and promotional support in the doctoral tract. Both of these cases were removed from the bill. I understand you, Professor Włodzimierz Bernacki, who emphasizes the role of the PiS in this area. I value him for many accomplishments, but I remind you that it was from this speaker that the most critical remarks came to the previous draft law on degrees, expressed by the representative of Kukiz.15. We are aware that arithmetic is in favour of the PiS club and that without its representatives' understanding the importance of the comments expressed would not change anything. We also believe that this will also be the case when we talk about systemic regulation of higher education matters, because it will certainly benefit smaller academic and urban centres in Poland, as all Members should consider. The proposed law in the proposed form is more valid, although as Kukiz,15 we cannot accept the powers left in it of the President of the Council of Ministers to elect the chairman of the Central Committee on Scientific Degrees. The project regulates the process of the academic career path to the degree of doctorate based on conducting research aimed at the practical application of them. It also establishes a programme under the heading "Mr. Implementation doctorate. It will allow the funding of costs related to scientific activities carried out in cooperation with the socio-economic environment, including the entrepreneur or other entity interested in a specific area of research, which will result in a PhD in implementation. Although this is not written directly in the project, it appears from the explanation of the Ministry of Science and Higher Education that basically the implementation doctorate can be carried out in respect of any area of public life. This means that representatives of social sciences, pedagogical sciences or humanities will also be able to apply for it. However, the problem is that this situation is reflected in the grants granted by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education to scholarships, which will depend on who will decide on the terms of the competition for this type of scholarship - in the practice of ministerial officials. Unfortunately, this situation raises some concerns, especially when consideration is given to the statement made by the Ministry's representative during the subcommittee's deliberations in which the contested law was examined. He said that the purpose of the implementation habilitation is to get people from abroad who can be promoters for implementation PhDs, because there are no such people in Poland. Although, fortunately, the provisions on obtaining allowances equivalent to habilitation have been removed, the attitude of the Ministry's representative raises some concerns about the transparency of the rules for granting scholarships from the point of view of accessibility for all fields of science. There is concern that certain areas may be marginalised. However, bearing in mind the obvious benefits for Polish science and society resulting from the provisions of the contested law, although its life will probably be short, because work is underway on the systemic regulation of the functioning of higher education, the Poselski Kukiz Club15 is in favour of its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The applicant proposes that the Prime Minister appoint the Chair of the Central Commission for Grades and Titles from among all its members, giving the Head of Government full freedom to elect its Chair. The Ministry, in turn, considers the wording of the Article to be a lack of such freedom. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3. I would like to remind you that at that time the Prime Minister appointed the chairman of the committee from among the two candidates submitted by the committee of its members. I will quote the amendment proposed by the Modern Club: the chairman of the Central Commission shall be appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers from two candidates submitted by the Central Committee of its members within 30 days of the date of submission of the nominations. So here we go. also a time limit for the selection of a member of the committee - 30 days. I will ask the question: What prevents the chairman of the committee from being elected since January - 3.5 months ago? I would like to remind you that the committee has been paralyzed for several months and is not functioning because the Prime Minister has not identified this chair from among the committee candidates. This solution should be seen as a step towards centralising the management of Polish science around one decision-making centre. To date, the Prime Minister's designation of two candidates as president was a safety valve, because the Prime Minister's influence on the functioning of the committee was not small. I would also like to point out that the way in which the chairman of the central committee is elected so far requires significant clarification. To date, the Act has not embarrassed the Head of Government as to the time in which he had to decide on the President's person. Thus, at the beginning of 2017, Prime Minister Beata Szydło did not appoint a chairman from among the candidates selected from the central committee. The High Seym, as a modern one, we have listened to the opinion of representatives of the scientific community when we examine this project. We have really listened to these opinions, also on the committee. They do not give us the mandate to unconditionally support the proposed amendment. On the basis of this law, scientists will be able to do research PhDs instead of habilitation, which will speed up the career path. We also know that the official idea is to create a bridge between business and science. Some say it's supposed to be a kind of transfer of knowledge to business. As we know, so far the doctoral dissertation must have been a new, original solution to some scientific problem. This is to change, to be dealt with more widely, because the hearing could also be an original, innovative solution for the application of own research results in the economic sphere. It's an interesting concept, because the theory is joined by practice. At the same time, the legislation still requires theoretical knowledge and the ability to carry out scientific work on its own. The shadow of the main objectives of the project raises doubts as to whether the change proposed by the government should balance the doctorate of research with habilitation. Many scientists fear that these changes can unexpectedly give rise to pathology regarding the feasibility of decisions relating to the granting of a PhD in implementation. The purpose of the bill is also the so-called. promotion grants granted discretionaryly. Promotors grant them to their wards. Wouldn't that be a pretext for creating a specific feudal system among scientists, including financial dependence? Political sympathies could also be involved. Unfortunately, we believe that this is a real threat to the project. If such a system were also to sneak into political premises, then it could mean promoting only those scientists who, as they say, follow the party line. It is certainly necessary to avoid politicising science, so also to blow on the cold, and also not to build systems that would favor such politicization. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you very much for all the comments and comments, including the critical ones we listened to carefully. Let me answer the questions you have asked. If I miss anything, I might add it in writing. In the order of the questions raised: Mr Bartosz Józwiak asked how much social sciences and humanities could be included in the implementation doctorate programme. This will depend on the activities of universities, which conduct social and humanities orientations, because the procedure for granting a PhD in implementation, and this also refers to critical comments, especially those that have alleged a possible politicisation of the whole process... I would like to make it very clear to you that implementation PhDs, just like any other PhDs, remain an exclusive domain of academic autonomy, i.e. they will be awarded by faculty councils. And neither the ministry, nor the minister, nor any member of the government will have any influence on what doctorate, when and by whom it will be defended. The role of the Ministry is limited to the fact that we are launching a competition for which universities apply. Colleges get resources that are then available among potential PhD students. So if they apply to the program, universities that conduct social sciences and humanities, then they will then be able to recruit students from these fields for doctoral studies and grant doctoral degrees in this area. In this case, a huge field of activity is left to universities. They're the ones who need to be active here. Mr Krzysztof Szulowski asked about the supporter's role as to whether he would duplicate the promoter. He will not duplicate the promoter, because the role of promoter is focused on maintaining and fulfilling all the conditions that are required for each doctorate. This is again a commentary on the critical voices that appeared during the presentation of club posts. Implementation PhDs are not other PhDs. The implementation PhDs are exactly the same PhDs, they must comply with the exact same scientific and methodological rigors, but they must have more. In this sense, They do not reduce the level of Polish science, they extend the scope of the science. The scientific guardian is not responsible for driving... Excuse me, the support officer is not responsible for conducting the scientific process. He is solely and exclusively responsible for overseeing the work of a PhD student, which will be carried out by an entrepreneur who will be involved in the scope of the research. As the name suggests, it is of a subsidiary nature and in no case must it enter into the power and competence of the promoter whose power over the scientific process will be complete. It was also a question of whether engineers should not be more able to develop their career paths, i.e. experienced engineers working in companies today. That's exactly what we were thinking about when we were preparing this program. Also about those who didn't finish their studies just a year ago, whether they might end up this year, but who maybe have already acquired a lot of experience. They have this practical knowledge, which they could now expand into theoretical knowledge, about building a theory. These people can also join this implementation doctorate program, of course, by applying to the right university, which has the authority to conduct doctoral degrees. That's what we're counting on in this case. Mrs Ewa Tomaszewska and Mr Stefan Strzałkowski touched on a similar issue, which is to what extent we have benefited from the experience of countries where implementation doctorates already exist. Yes, we did. And here I would like to say to you that this project is a project, which follows from the scientific community's demands that such an implementation doctorate as has been in place in Denmark since 1971 - since 1971. Danes have their implementation PhDs well developed - or what it is like in France, where they have their implementation PhDs since 1978... Young Polish scientists who went abroad and who came back at the meetings claimed: please introduce a similar mechanism, non-revolutionist, only supporting those who study at the interface of universities and the economy, science and the economy. So we took advantage of these experiences. These are also the experiences that the European Commission is trying to recommend to all countries today. So that we are one of the European countries that is trying to catch up as soon as possible in this area. This programme will meet with great approval and he meets with great approval from the academic community, as well as those who will do these PhDs. And this criticism in the environment is, in my opinion, far less than what has been said, in particular by your opposition Members. On the other hand, I understand that this remark refers to the frequently appearing thread of the central accreditation committee. And here I would just like to draw your attention to the fact that a procedure similar to the one proposed in this law is used in the case of the Polish Accreditation Commission, i.e. the so-called. PAK, where not the Prime Minister, but the Minister appoints a chairman from among all members who are elected, posted to work in the Polish Accreditation Board. In this case, we are also not violating the autonomy or self-government of the scientific community, because all members of the central accreditation committee are elected. So each of them has potential or opportunity. And this is rather an extension of trust in the academic community. I don't see, we don't see any important elements here that would be centralized. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u34	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'm handing over the information that comes from the meeting of the Committee on Infrastructure. Postal law as regards the method of financing the net cost of universal service obligations to the recommendations of the European Commission. We have these recommendations from the European Commission, and that is what we are doing right now. The project introduces major changes in the way the postal operator's contribution to the net cost financing of universal service obligations by the designated operator is calculated and requires the President of the Office of Electronic Communications to carry out a regular market survey in order to determine the adequacy of the thresholds for the operator's contribution to the payment and the level of that contribution. In addition, the project makes the mechanism for launching grants for public mail exempt from postal charges more flexible. The problem is that Poczta Polska is the designated operator. It happens that Polish Post has to provide services that are not always adequate to the costs incurred. Therefore, as a committee, we recommend solutions that will allow Poczta Polska to simply adopt these solutions, which are safe for the functioning, financial functioning of the Polish Post. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u35	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I ask Mr Peter King, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u36	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present our position on the draft law amending the Postal Law Act, Parliamentary Printing No. 1426. Since this is the first change of the Postal Law in this term, I wouldn't be myself if I didn't recall a few numbers. In 2007, the number of post offices that were in Poland was 8692 - these data that I am giving you, I am based on the reports of the Office of Electronic Communications of 2007 and 2015. - Whereas in 2015 the number of offices fell to 7387. Within a few years, the Civic Platform and PSL have closed down nearly 1000 post offices. It is also worth looking at employment in the case of the largest postal operator, employment in Poczta Polska. I would like to remind you, also on the basis of the data resulting from the reports of the Office for Electronic Communications, that this employment was still over 100,000 in 2007. employees and dropped to 75 000 in 2015. employment. This shows how deep the crisis under the last PO-PSL coalition went through Poland Post. This project, which we are talking about today, concerns the alignment of the current solutions of the Law of 12 November 2012. Postal law as regards the method of financing the net cost of universal service obligations with the recommendations of the European Commission. The recommendations were made in the decision approving the financing from the point of view of compliance with the State aid rules and competition rules. The Commission's decision concerned compensation for the designated operator - Poczta Polska for the net cost of universal service obligations for the years 2013 to 2015. This financing, which we are talking about today, will take place in a situation where the designated operator will suffer a loss for the provision of universal services. The calculation of the net cost shall be designed to determine whether the financial result of the designated operator is adversely affected by the fulfilment of the obligations imposed. The net cost shall be determined by comparing the actual financial result of the incumbent operator with the hypothetical situation in which that operator would be exempt from that obligation and could freely modify the scope and way in which its activities are carried out, following only the criterion of maximising the economic profit. Where a positive net cost is found, the operator shall be entitled to obtain compensation, which constitutes State aid, since it consists of: firstly, universal service providers and falling within the scope of universal service, and secondly, complementaryly, when the measures collected from operators prove insufficient, the State budget. Therefore, the compensation required notification in order to establish its compatibility with the internal market. The amendments to the Act also concern the adjustment of the method of determining the proportion of postal operators' contribution to net cost financing. The current share is calculated on the total revenue of the operator from universal services and services falling within the scope of universal services. After the change, the revenues of the operator up to PLN 1 million will not be taken into account. This is the threshold from which the obligation to participate in the financing of this cost arises. At the same time, the contribution may not be higher than 2% That's the revenue. In practice, this means that all operators, both with revenues below PLN 1 million and higher revenues, will not have a contribution from the first million PLN. Maximum share level of 2% revenue, remains unchanged. This draft also provides for penalties to be imposed on postal operators for failure to provide information and for the provision of false or incomplete information necessary for a fair examination. The President of UKE will inform postal operators of the threat of financial penalties for failure to comply with information obligations. At the same time, the current penalty imposed by the President of UKE on an operator designated for failure to submit the documents necessary to determine the net cost and loss for universal services has been waived, as they will have to be submitted in conjunction with the request of the operator designated for payment according to the project. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice intends to support this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It should be stressed that, in line with the Commission's view, the introduction of the programme notification procedure for the following years, de facto determining the implementation of the provisions of the Law in this respect. As the consequence of the proposed law is the occurrence of budgetary implications, according to the Public Finance Act, a limit of budgetary expenditure has been set for each subsequent year for a period of 10 years starting from the date of entry into force of the provisions of this Law. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u62	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer the Members' questions. Mr Warzecha asked about the checks and the amount of the penalty. The draft provides for a maximum penalty, while its amount will be fixed by the President of the Office of Electronic Communications, taking into account the extent of the offence. The law only provides for maximum rates. As far as 300% of the average salary is concerned, I think that these are not drastic rates for individuals who violate the rules, but for entrepreneurs, they are already hundreds, up to 300,000. Euros, so they're bigger. Of course, the President of UKE will take into account both the scale of the offence and its nature as regards the application of the penalty. Now with regard to questions directly unrelated to the law, but concerning it indirectly may also, because it is the cost of the universal service, it can first monitor and track the consignment. We want the shipments to go as soon as possible. Of course it is. also the main task of mail to carry out these shipments in a timely manner. In the case of certain consignments, the post office is required to carry them out within D+1 and in this respect it is controlled by the Office of Electronic Communications whether this timeliness is implemented. On the other hand, the possibility of tracking a registered shipment, because we are talking about it, already exists. You can track the package on Envelo's platform. It is the platform of the company of the daughter of Poczta Polska - Posta Polska Digital Services is a company which operates the electronic platform of Envelo, and on this platform you can follow the registered shipment. Scanning shipments is possible. Already at the moment, Poland Post offers so-called. Hybrid service, that someone can at the post office present the type of letter or mail in paper form, mail can scan it and send it to the recipient in electronic form and vice versa, someone can send it in electronic form, Polish Post will print it and send it to the recipient in paper form. The limited liability company itself is based on the trust of the people or entities that the company is establishing. And, of course, not only in these cases and not only for this reason, there was a lot of irregularities in setting up a limited liability company in the past, but I do not know if it is the best idea to model a way to achieve the desired goal. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u90	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Code of Commercial Companies Act, the Code of Civil Procedure Act and the Act on the National Court Register, print no. 1230. The aim of this project is to eliminate the possibility of using a simple electronic signature when setting up S-24 limited liability companies and thereby improve the safety of legal trading. To sign the agreement of the company with the o.o. only a qualified electronic signature and a signature confirmed by a trusted profile of ePUAP would be used to apply for such a company to the National Court Register. Since 1 January 2012, it is possible to establish a company with o.o. using the template made available in the electronic system, mode S-24, and applying to the signing of the contract and to submit an application for entry in the National Court Register for an ordinary electronic signature. It was introduced to stimulate entrepreneurship and facilitate business activity in this most popular corporate form. This solution proved to be a great success. Number of companies in O.O. assumed in the S-24 mode is steadily growing and currently account for almost half of all newly created companies. In total between January 2012 and November 2015, 36 678 companies were established in this manner. One of the elements that decided to give entrepreneurs a great interest in using the S-24 mode to establish the company was the uncomplicated solution for users with regard to electronic signature required to sign the contract and submit an application to the KRS. According to estimates, almost 80% users use a simple electronic signature. Such a signature is sufficient to establish an account in the electronic system for handling the attachment and other activities concerning the company and to activate the signature in the system where a relatively limited number of data is required: name, PESEL number and address for correspondence. However, during the last few months there have been signals about cases of abuse involving the unauthorised use of personal data of other persons to establish a company from o.o. in S-24 mode. Such situations may arise due to the fact that the use of an ordinary electronic signature in this case does not give 100% certainty that the person signing the contract is indeed the person who has been designated as having an agreement with the company. The system only checks the compatibility of the PESEL number indicated by the user with the data contained in the PESEL register and, in the case of foreigners who do not have a PESEL number, it is not possible to carry out even such verification. The authors of the project, giving priority to the safety of trading and in order to eliminate solutions that may lead to abuse, propose to withdraw from the ordinary electronic signature when setting up a company with o.o. and allow only qualified e-signatures or signatures confirmed by a trusted ePUAP profile to be used in this respect. High Izbo! All, even the most beneficial solutions and novelties, require timely review and improvement. The qualified electronic signature will be in the case of an important activity such as the establishment of companies with o.o. a much safer and more secure solution than a simple electronic signature. By building an e-state in which a citizen can easily handle official matters via the Internet, we must not forget about the safety of trading and the protection of personal data. Although the changes involve obstacles for entrepreneurs, in our view, as proposed by the projectors, they will contribute to improving the safety of legal trading.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u93	Thank you very much, Mr President, I am opening the discussion and ask you to speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u94	Thank you very much, Marshal. I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. As I have already pointed out, this amendment is the implementation of the request made in the petition P9 95 of the Law of 30 August 2002. The law on proceedings before administrative courts, which provides that at a public hearing only the persons called to the court have access to the courtroom outside the parties and persons called upon, and only the persons called to the court have access to the court in secret. The petitioner calls for an addition to the article. 95 § 2 Act on proceedings before administrative courts, stating that the court may authorise the presence of a minor who has completed 13. A year old. It should be stressed that during the work on the draft of this amendment there was a discussion, and we also gathered opinions from a number of institutions. It should be stressed that the majority of the institutions either did not express an opinion or that these opinions were positive. Only in the opinion of the Supreme Court did some comment expressing that the presence of a minor in the courtroom might make it difficult to hold a hearing. I would like to stress that during the work on the proposal for this amendment, in principle all parliamentary clubs supported this initiative. Also during the discussion with the representatives of the Public Prosecutor's Office, we considered that this amendment would have a positive impact on minors whose participation in the hearings would be educational. This may have a positive impact on the legal awareness of minors, the way in which the hearing is conducted, the approximation of the legal provisions, the approximation of justice to minors in general. In conclusion, since the opinion of the Minister of Justice was positive here and other institutions such as the Supreme Administrative Court or the National Council of the Court of Justice did not make any comments here, the committee, in principle unanimously, with one abstention, considered this amendment to be justified, the Parliamentary Law and Justice Club supports the proposed amendment. It is also worth noting that since 1998 the provision on the participation of minors in meetings with the agreement of the chairman of the formation of the court has been in force in the criminal procedure. That's what Art talks about. Article 356 (2) of the Code of Criminal Procedure, which provides that the President may authorise the presence of minors at the trial. It seems that, since this type of regulation unreservedly operates in a criminal procedure, it is all the more appropriate for it to also appear in a civil and judicial procedure. In my opinion, there is no danger of breaching the rights of the parties as to privacy or any information which the parties would not like to inform more about, since the decision to allow the minor to participate as a public at the hearing will always be the responsibility of the President, who will ensure that the proceedings are conducted as a whole. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'll answer your questions. Mrs Tomaszewska asks about specific categories of cases, in particular family matters. I think that the danger that you rightly point to is not the case, because both the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code and the Rules of the Law on proceedings before administrative courts guarantee recognition of cases of a particular nature at closed doors, i.e. without the participation of the public. So this, of course, is a matter of judging the judge. Moreover, in civil proceedings, in this area where the honourable Member rightly points out, cases for divorce and separation are officially recognised at the closed doors, i.e. those in which circumstances from private and family life may most often arise, or which threaten morality. It's an art. 427 Code of Civil Procedure. In other custody cases concerning minors, the court shall, of its own motion, administer the entire sitting or part of it at the closed door if the benefit of the minor is against public recognition of the case. And that's the basic determinant. It's an art. 575 Code of Civil Procedure. So I think that the risks that you point to are not taking place. With regard to Mr Tomczak's question on other countries of the European Union, how this issue is dealt with, we did not, of course, examine them all, but we have examined a few. For example, in Slovenia it is unacceptable for minors to be present at hearings, in Lithuania it is permissible for minors over 16 years of age. However, the presence of minors in France, Belgium, the Czech Republic, Slovakia or Malta is permitted without any age restrictions. There is no regulation, which means that it is also in principle permissible for minors to be present at hearings in Germany and Portugal. With regard to Mr Masłowski's question, whether it would be inappropriate to provide for minors, children of adults during the hearing, we have of course wondered about this idea, there has already been this doubt, but ultimately the government has taken the view that there is no need to do so. It will always be the judge's assessment whether, due to the nature of the case, the age of those who wish to participate will be acceptable or not. The introduction of such a criterion of adult care would in fact lead to a restriction on access to the real work of the court, because such care could not always be provided. This would, of course, also be a question of legitimacy, checking, some procedures, we would need to establish procedures. And rightly Mrs Chrobak mentioned that it was advisable for young people to get acquainted in a free way, with certain limitations, of course, which I was talking about, with the real work of the courts, not with what is presented as the work of the court on the TV talk show. Because it can only be for the benefit of these young people when they grow up and find themselves in a court of law, they won't be surprised, they'll really know how the third power works. So that we didn't want to restrict the Code of Criminal Procedure here. They're not here today. No one's reporting any problems because of it. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u110	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party to present our position on the report of the Senate draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and the Law on proceedings before administrative courts, print Nos 1248 and 1453. The aim of this project is to allow minors to participate in open court meetings. In any event, the court's consent to the participation of a minor would be necessary. The draft implements the petition submitted to the Senate in March 2016. The petitioner pointed out that, in the current legal state, it is authorised, with the President's consent, to the presence of minors in the courtroom only in criminal proceedings. However, there are many issues that are important to the community in the courts and the observation of public meetings would have educational advantages and would prepare minors to participate actively in public life. In the opinion of the draftsman, such a solution ensures the freedom of decision of the chairman of the formation of the court, who will take an individual decision, taking into account inter alia. the smooth course of the meeting or the possible demoralisation of the minor by the content presented during the meeting. The provision is also intended to allow parents and children with whom there is no other care to enter the courtroom, e.g. The parent shall take part in the hearing as a party or as a witness. According to the draft, the authorisation will be granted in the form of an order from which it will have recourse to the court of judgment. The provisions on the authorisation of a minor to participate in a public hearing issued outside the hearing will be open to challenge, but the chairman may revoke or amend them if the circumstances of the case change. Giving minors the right to be present in public at public hearings in civil proceedings before a general court and in administrative matters decided by an administrative court shall be fully supported by the Ombudsman of the rights of the child. On the other hand, the Supreme Court, in its opinion, takes the view that children's access to court should be restricted for their benefit. In addition, the presence of children in the courtroom undermines the seriousness of the court and may affect the quality of the explanations or testimony of the parent. According to the Supreme Court, a minor should have access to the courtroom after a certain age, as an example of 16 years of age. High Izbo! In our view, allowing minors to attend court meetings will strengthen the subjectivity of children, enabling them to participate actively in public life, to gain knowledge and experience in civil and administrative matters. It will also help to shape respect for the law and strengthen the sense of justice in children. The proposed change is the implementation of the right of the child to information, which is essential to meet the educational needs, the school obligation or the preparation of the child for an active life in civil society. Referring to the Supreme Court's arguments regarding the protection of the child's well-being: we believe that the child's well-being will be sufficiently protected by a view of the President's judgment, which will each issue a decision on the entry of minors into the courtroom.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u120	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present my position on the parliamentary draft law amending the Fire Protection Act following the presentation of a report by the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs. These are 1412 and 1448 prints. High Izbo! Dear Friends! The blade of the sword, which is the bill tabled by the PiS Members, is aimed directly at the selfless service of many hundreds of thousands of volunteer firefighters to improve the security of their fellow citizens. In essence, the proposal concerns the amendment of the Article. 38 and Article 38 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Currently, these articles require insurance companies to transfer 10% the sum of the proceeds obtained from the compulsory fire insurance for fire protection purposes and specify how these funds are to be distributed in such a way that half of the funds transferred are available to the Chief of the State Fire Service and the other half to the General Board of the Association of Volunteer Fire Guards of the Republic of Poland. Independently of the authorising officer, the measures may be transferred only for the purpose of fire protection and detailed rules for the allocation of measures have been defined by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs by means of a regulation of 2 June 1999. You have to say it straight: it works well. Unfortunately, PiS MEPs, striking at volunteer firefighters, want to change this matter and an effective system. In turn, the change will consist in the fact that from the moment of entry into force of the procedated provisions on the distribution of funds will be decided exclusively and personally by the chief commander of the State Fire Service. This wrong proposal, I have already said it, should be a clear and clear assessment. Firstly, this bill is a very strong and clear expression of distrust towards hundreds of thousands of volunteer firefighters who perform their selfless service to improve the security of their fellow citizens. The statutory powers for the allocation of funds which were intended, which should be stressed, have been taken away only for the purpose of fire protection, i.e. they could not be spent on other purposes. They have been spent in a way that takes into account the needs of our local homelands, our cities, our villages, our villages. This is the project that needs to be assessed very negatively. The day when this scandalous bill will be passed will be another black day when the PiS will show that it has the selfless service of volunteer firefighters for common security. Secondly, this is another step towards centralising the state. It is an expression of blind faith in the fact that PiS politicians who are sitting in Warsaw know better what citizens need throughout Poland. It is ignoring the needs of local countries and the right to make them. Moreover, the proposed way of centralising the allocation of funds raises a clear concern in the context of the experience of the PiS governments so far that the distribution of these funds will be determined by a political factor rather than a substantive one. Thirdly, the changes are carried out within a year, which is obviously confusing with plans that have so far been drawn up on the basis of existing legislation. It's like the law's working backwards. Finally, I would like to emphasise once again that this law tabled by the PiS Members is the blade of the sword aimed directly at the selfless service of many hundreds of thousands of volunteer firefighters to improve the security of their fellow citizens. This is not the case and there will be no modern consent. In view of the above, the Posel Club The modern proposal to reject the bill at second reading, as we did at first reading. If we reject our proposal, we make amendments. The adoption of these amendments will mean that decisions on how funds are spent will continue to be made by those who are selflessly concerned about our security, namely the voluntary firefighters. I encourage the High House to do so regardless of the party indications. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u170	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia wheels, I present my position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Fire Protection Act. In accordance with the provisions in force so far, insurance undertakings are required to transfer 10% the amount collected for compulsory fire insurance for fire protection units. Half of this amount is now transferred to the Chief of the State Fire Service and the other half to the Union of Voluntary Fire Guards of the Republic of Poland, which distributes it between individual units of the TSO. This draft law provides for changes in the distribution of funds transferred from insurance companies. According to the proposed regulation, the sole authorising officer of these measures would be the head of the PSP. As the applicants point out, the proposed changes will serve to make the financial resources concerned more efficient and to control them more effectively. I agree that, in the light of the irregularities mentioned at the committee meeting, such as the previous irregularities. overestimation of the costs of the equipment purchased or discrimination in the allocation of non-union TSOs funds, changes in this area are necessary. However, my doubts are raised by some of the novels. This is true of the fact that, according to the new regulations, the financial resources currently received by TSOs from the relationship cannot be spent for the same purposes as before but can only be spent for the so-called. ensuring combat preparedness. It is not enough for me to ensure that these entities also now continue not only with the funding that we are talking about, but also with subsidies from self-funded sponsors, from their events or from sales made in their shops. However, I think that this is still a matter for discussion and refinement. I am convinced that the transfer of funds by insurance companies to one entity and not to two so far will translate into a more even separation between the various units of the voluntary fire department. However, due to the objections raised, the Free and Solidarnia area will request that this project be re-worked in committee.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u172	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, I present a report on the Government's draft law on claims for damages caused by a breach of competition law, print No 1370. High Izbo! First of all, I wanted to briefly present the main objectives of this project. First of all, it should be recalled that this is the implementation of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council 104/2014, so-called. Compensation Directive of 26 November 2014 The government proposal for the implementation of this directive was prepared in such a way that the government decided on a separate law and amendment in the trade union rules, i.e. the Civil Code, the Civil Procedure Code, the Act on Combating Unfair Competition of 16 April 1993 and the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection of 16 February 2017. First of all, the matter of this law, it should also be emphasized, concerns the private legal sphere. To date, it can be said that Polish entrepreneurs did not have the right legal matter in terms of such claims to remedy the damage caused by the infringement of competition law, especially in relation to foreign entities. It is included in the article. 2 The case concerning the dictionary of legal terms, there are new concepts, previously unknown in the Polish legal system, such as the concept of cartel, direct buyer, indirect buyer, is also the concept of an entrepreneur within the meaning of this law. Procedural provisions are also introduced. Namely, as regards property, it will be appropriate for district courts regardless of the value of the subject matter of the dispute. The rules also govern the procedure, new institutions are introduced, which have not yet been known in civil proceedings. I mean revealing the evidence, i.e. Such a request may be made by the parties to the proceedings. The court may also order the defendant, third parties and the competition authority to do so. Appropriate orders are provided for or provisions are made to dismiss this request for disclosure, of course certain conditions must be met, and this is regulated in Article. 20. The application for disclosure of evidence shall be subject to a complaint. As far as other issues relating to the effectiveness of this procedure are concerned, I would like to stress that Article. 25 introduces rules on the effectiveness of the provisions requiring disclosure of the measure in the procedure of enforcement of non-monetary benefits, i.e. refers to Article. 1050 and Article 1050 1050 Code of Civil Procedure, where a one-off fine of up to 10 000 is foreseen. PLN and the total amount of fines measured may be up to PLN 1 million. There are also additional sanctions when a person evades disclosure of evidence as a party to the proceedings, e.g. destroys the document. There are specific procedural consequences. A financial penalty for filing an application for disclosure of evidence in bad faith - here is a fine of up to 20,000. zł. It is not uncommon for such requests to be made to postpone the proceedings. The General Court shall be bound by a final decision of the competition authorities of other Member States of the European Union. As regards the determination of the amount of the damage, here, the courts should be assisted by the guidelines of the European Commission, the courts may also apply to the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection or to the competition authorities of another Member State in order to properly determine this amount of compensation. This is quite difficult and complicated. As a last resort, Article 1 shall remain applicable. 322 Code of Civil Procedure and this compensation, of course, if it is excessively difficult - then the court decides it at its own discretion. The substantive provisions concerning the limitation of these claims have also been amended, which is very important. This period has been extended from the current 3-year limitation period to 5 years, except that the beginning of the limitation period is calculated once the infringement of competition law has ceased. As far as the procedure is concerned, the joint committees: the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights have set up an emergency subcommittee which has introduced three substantive amendments to this project. The first of these substantive amendments concerns an amendment to the Article. 8. In the original project, the interest rate was determined as the compensatory percentage, i.e. the reference to the bank reference rate, i.e. 1,5% interest. The Commission introduced substitute statutory interest here, which is currently at 5% so it is particularly beneficial for those who are pursuing these claims. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 The limitation period shall be suspended as soon as the investigation is initiated. This investigation has been carried out on several occasions in the case of in rem It lasts for a few years. It's hard to say that unless the perpetrator of the infringement is established, it was a statute of limitations. Thus, as I have already said, this limitation is only calculated from the determination of the cessation of the infringement of competition law. The third major amendment that has been introduced concerns transitional periods. The point is that the provisions of this Act apply in the case of claims for damages caused by an infringement of competition law which occurred after its entry into force. Here, there is not an important time limit for initiating proceedings, but a breach of competition law. In such a case, if the infringement occurred before the entry into force of that law, the provisions of that law are to apply, and if the proceedings were initiated earlier, the existing provisions shall apply, no matter when the infringement of competition law occurred. As far as vacathio legis is concerned, a 14-day minimum period has been maintained. It is beneficial for those who wish to bring such actions. The aim is to ensure that the whole range of new rights of the parties submitting such proceedings can be applied as soon as possible. On the other hand, the deadline for transposing this compensation directive, which has already expired since it was 27 December 2016, indicates that such legal solutions should be implemented as soon as possible. Let me just add that 10 Member States of the European Union have already introduced this directive. Both the Emergency Sub-Committee and the Joint Committees: the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, whose joint meeting took place yesterday, have in principle unanimously supported this proposal, with one abstention. I move that the High Sejm also adopt the government draft of this law, which is definitely needed by Polish entrepreneurs. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u184	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u186	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law on claims for damages caused by the infringement of competition law. This project regulates and standardizes the liability for damages caused by a breach of competition law and the principle of redress in civil proceedings. The proposal also aims to implement the EU Parliament and Council Directive of 26 November 2014 on certain provisions governing the enforcement of damages claims against Member States and the European Union's competition law. The Compensation Directive lays down the provisions necessary to ensure that any person who has suffered damage resulting from an infringement of EU competition law or national competition law by an undertaking or a association of undertakings, an effective investigation of that undertaking or a link between the full compensation for the damage suffered. The aim of the Act is to optimise relations between the public and private mechanisms for implementing competition law and to ensure that the full right to remedy the damage suffered by victims of an infringement of EU competition rules is guaranteed. The project assumes that the perpetrator of the infringement is obliged to make good the damage caused to anyone by the infringement of competition law, unless he is not guilty. According to this provision, the trader will have to prove that he is not guilty. In addition, the draft law provides that the infringement of competition law is detrimental. This presumption is intended to make it easier for the injured party to demonstrate the liability of the perpetrator of the infringement. An important change is to introduce in the Civil Code a provision that the limitation period does not begin and is suspended for the duration of mediation or other out-of-court means of resolving the dispute concerning the claim, unless otherwise provided. In addition, in the event that the limitation is interrupted by proceedings before a court or other body called upon to recognise or enforce a claim of a type or an arbitration tribunal, the limitation shall not run again until the proceedings have been completed. The Act is intended to cover the recovery of claims for damages caused by any infringement of competition law, regardless of whether the infringement affects the Union market or only the domestic market. This applies both to restrictive agreements, in particular cartels established by competitive companies, as well as to unauthorised unilateral practices of dominant parties. The victims of a breach of competition law shall be entitled to full compensation for the damage caused by the infringement, together with interest of a compensatory nature from the date on which the damage occurred to the date on which the compensation was due. The draft provides that cases for remedying the damage caused by the infringement of competition law will fall within the jurisdiction of the district courts regardless of the value of the matter at issue. This is supported by a high degree of complexity in these cases, especially in cases where the enforcement of an action is not preceded by a decision of the competition authority to recognise a particular practice as restrictive of competition, their relationship with the public-law enforcement of competition law, as well as specific arrangements in the field of evidence proceedings which constitute a novel in Polish civil proceedings. The bill introduces a specific and unknown type of application in the Polish civil procedure: a request for disclosure of evidence. In order to provide the injured party with the right to full compensation required by the Directive, it is provided that, if the court determines the amount of compensation at prices from a date other than the date on which the compensation is established, the injured party will also be entitled to interest at the reference rate of the NBP for the period from the date on which the prices were the basis for establishing the compensation until the date on which the claim for damages was due. In general terms, the court will also have the opportunity to adjudicate an appropriate sum at its own discretion when strict proof of the amount of the request is impossible or extremely difficult. The draft assumes that the determination of the final decision of the President of the OKiK on the recognition of the practice as restrictive of competition or the final judgment of the court given as a result of an appeal against such a decision will bind the court in proceedings for the recovery of the damage caused by the infringement of competition law as regards the finding of an infringement of competition law. High Izbo! There is no doubt that there is a need for better protection in the common EU market against infringement of competition law. Both the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of the EU and this proposal are moving in this direction. To date, due to the high level of complexity of cases, the difficulties of evidence and the frequent imbalance in the market position between the perpetrator of the infringement and the victim of relatively few victims have decided to make a claim for damages. The proposed law will introduce a number of facilitations in the recovery of claims, thus reducing the gap between the perpetrator and the injured party in favour of the latter.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u210	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Cinema and some other laws, prints Nos 1427 and 1444. The main purpose of the project submitted by the Government of the Republic of Poland is to amend the Act of 30 June 2005 on cinematography. The proposed proposal amends the provisions in such a way as to ensure consistency. Act of 25 October 1991 on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities and the resulting powers of the organisers of cultural activities. The project envisages replacing the name of the cultural institution currently mentioned in the Act of 2005 on cinematography, i.e. National Film Library, a more general formulation indicating the existence of an institution of culture specialised in the protection of national cultural heritage in the field of cinematography. It is also important that, in view of the need to ensure greater efficiency of actions to safeguard and make available the national audiovisual heritage, with particular regard to the collections of the National Film Library and the potential of the knowledge and technology of the National Audiovisual Institute, a combination of these cultural institutions is planned on the basis of the provisions of the Act of 25 October 1991 on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities. And most importantly, as a result of the draft law submitted by the government of the merger, a state institution of culture will be created: National Film Library - Audiovisual Institute. In view of the fact that the collection and protection of the Polish filmmaking acquis in accordance with the provisions of the 2005 Act on cinematography remains the task indicated in the Act under the name of the National Film Library, it is necessary to make appropriate changes to this act allowing for the change of name and the merger of two cultural institutions. It is, of course, the National Film Library and the National Audiovisual Institute. At the meeting of the Committee on Culture and Media held on 5 April 2017, Members asked a number of questions. A substantive discussion has been held. The future of this new combined cultural institution has been addressed. They were asked about substantive, personal matters, as well as the collections of the National Audiovisual Institute. In response, Minister Jarosław Sellin reassured that it would actually be such a strengthening of the activities of these two institutions after the merger that there would be synergies, that the budget, rights, experience of employees, all this would continue by the new institution of culture, if the Polish Parliament adopted it, and that as much as possible the collections of the National Audiovisual Institute would be safe under the new, revised law. One amendment to the proposed law, as well as various legislative amendments, was subsequently adopted as a result of the vote. Finally, in the vote of the members of the Committee on Culture and Media, 12 votes in favour and 9 votes against the draft law was positively recommended to the High House. I would like to remind you that this is about the merger of the National Film Library and the National Audiovisual Institute and the creation of a national culture institution specialized in the protection of national cultural heritage in the field of cinematography. This new institution will be called: National Film Library - Audiovisual Institute. On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I recommend that the High House adopt a draft government law amending the Cinema Act and certain other laws. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u238	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour, on behalf of the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, to present the report of the committee, which is included in print No 1469, which concerns the draft of the European Convention on Violence and Excesses of spectators in the course of sports events, in particular football matches, drawn up in Strasbourg on 19 August 1985. I would like to say that we are proposing that, without any change, the Sejm should approve the project that has been submitted. It should be said briefly that this convention has become obsolete because in 2016 a new draft convention was adopted at the Council of Europe Delegation Committee, which will be discussed in the next item, so I will not quote it. As you know from the title, this convention, which is still in force today, was adopted by us in 1985 and is still in force today. With years, however, as we all know, some issues have become obsolete, some new challenges have emerged, and there is a new technology also fighting in this area. This is all contained in the new legal decisions of the European countries. This old convention was ratified by 42 countries in a specific situation. We remember all the events that really upset us in 1985, the tragedy on Heysel. Today, a large part, as I said, is obsolete and incompatible with the current European experience and good practices that are applied in these countries. The focus on the violence of fans in isolation from these other factors, the security systems, the protection of viewers at football stadiums themselves is too narrowly covered in the current document. The Convention also runs counter to some of the recommendations made by the Council of Europe's Permanent Committee over the last 30 years, which undermines the credibility of the functioning of the Convention. The provisions of the Convention, which we want to express, do not reflect the current perspective of looking at security in sport, namely the need for an integrated multi-subject system of security management and support of sports viewers and the need for dialogue and cooperation with football fans and other, of course, sports disciplines. Changing the content of the Convention will allow for the implementation of new ways to combat aggression, life-threatening incidents and health-threatening sporting events. The interesting thing is that we went so far that the new document is not only about what's going on at the stadium, but also about the dangers on the way to the stadium, what are the threats in places outside the stadium, where fans meet in large groups to watch, through television signal, sports events, football matches, in so-called. fan zones. These are all new experiences, and that is what we must respond to today in line with the experiences of recent years. Therefore, in 2012, with the large participation of the Polish Minister of Sport, work began on a new convention which, as I said, ended last year. This document will better serve international cooperation, between countries. So that the recommendation of the joint committee is unanimous. We would like to propose to adopt a government bill on the termination of the European Convention on Violence and Excesses of spectators at sporting events, and in particular football matches, drawn up in Strasbourg on 19 August 1985. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u246	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present on behalf of the Poselski Club a state-of-the-art position on the government draft law on the termination of the European Convention on Violence and Excesses of spectators during sporting events, in particular football matches, drawn up in Strasbourg on 19 August 1985, following the presentation of a report by the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism and the Committee on Foreign Affairs. These are 1407 and 1469 prints. High Izbo! This draft law, as I mentioned, concerns the termination of the European Convention on Safety at sporting events. It was drawn up in 1985. Almost 10 years later, Poland ratified the Convention and committed itself to applying its provisions on 1 June 1995. Of course, as you can imagine, the contents of the convention reflected the existing views and practices at the time regarding modeling the way the audience behaves during sporting events. The Convention highlighted the role of crowd management through the use of barriers and fences and the activities of law enforcement and information services. However, with the progress made over the last two decades in securing sporting events, with the emergence of new challenges, but also the creative approach to safety management, this convention has become obsolete. It is worth noting that some of its records are in contradiction with the current practice and contemporary experience of the organizers of sporting events. In the light of the above conclusions, a proposal for a new Council of Europe Convention on an integrated approach to safety, security and handling during football and other sporting events was adopted at the Council of Europe's Committee of Delegates of Ministers on 4 May 2016. This new convention responds to today's challenges in this area and aims to replace the current Convention. However, Poland's commitment to the new convention will only be possible if the 1985 Convention is terminated and this draft law is satisfied. In the light of the facts cited, the termination of the present Convention should be considered as appropriate and, therefore, as a result of the above considerations, the Posel Club The modern bill will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u258	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present on behalf of the Poselski Club a modern position on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Council of Europe Convention on an integrated approach to security, security and service during football matches and other sports events, drawn up in Strasbourg on 4 May 2016, signed by the Minister of Sport and Tourism of the Republic of Poland on 29 November 2016, following the presentation of a report by the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, print No 1408 and 1470. High Izbo! This bill concerns the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Council of Europe Convention, which I mentioned just now. This is a new international agreement, which aims, as stated in the explanatory memorandum, to prevent incidents and disturbances of public order that pose a threat to the lives and health of people participating in football matches and other sporting events. The scope of the matters covered by this new agreement has so far been partly regulated in the European Convention on Violence and Excesses of spectators during sporting events, and in particular football matches, from 1985, but because the Convention did not meet the contemporary challenges, a new agreement has been drawn up which is the subject of this debate. At the same time, as we know, there is a procedure in the Sejm for the termination of the previous convention. The new Convention introduces a number of changes taking into account contemporary knowledge of the safety of sporting events. Here we should mention, for example, the holistic, integrated, system-based concept of safety management, as opposed to the individualisation of tasks so far, or the emphasis in the new Convention on the importance of properly trained cleaning and information services. The new agreement also draws attention to the importance of risk estimation and the implementation of preventive measures as a necessary attribute of a safe sporting event. And again, this is a positive distinction between the present Convention and the previous approach, i.e. direct interventions that may be a trigger and cause an escalation of violence. A new element in the Convention Regulatory Area is also an indication of the importance of cooperation with fans and the importance of proper service of the sports viewer. They're anti-aggression factors. In the light of the facts cited, ratification of the Convention should be considered to be deliberate. In view of the above, the Posel Club Modern consents to ratification by the President of the Republic of Poland of the Convention and will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Standing Committee of the Council of Europe has developed a proposal for a new Council of Europe Convention on an integrated approach to safety, security and handling during football and other sporting events. The Convention describes the basic principles, assumptions and security measures based on the current state of knowledge and good practices. The new Convention reflects the view that focusing solely on threats does not lead to effective risk reduction. A more effective solution is the implementation of both security and security measures as well as the proper service of viewers. Both factors strongly affect the behaviour of fans. The overarching principle of the Convention is an integrated multi-party approach to security, based on three pillars: security, security and service, and cooperation between all other institutions and stakeholders. The new Council of Europe Convention introduces innovative solutions to improve the security of mass events and the service of their viewers. Mr. Marshal! The Sejm at its 35th meeting on 9 February 2017 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the draft to the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism for consideration. One of the main objectives of the project is the establishment of the Polish Anti-Doping Agency, POLADA, instead of the present Commission for Combating Doping in Sport. Giving the new agency the status of a state legal person supervised by the Minister of Sport and Tourism offers more opportunities to solve the problems of the way in which the institution is financed, which should deal with the fight against doping. This will also allow for additional funding for ongoing activities related to the fight against doping in both professional and mass sports - children, young people and adults. The project allows for the exchange of information and close cooperation between police officers, Border Guards, Military Gendarmerie or National Tax Administration. This will enable the Agency to conduct its own doping investigations effectively. The authors of the project also provide for the creation of a Disciplinary Panel at the agency to settle matters concerning doping in sport. This is undoubtedly an improvement in the quality of case law in the application of illegal doping. So far, Polish sports unions have ruled on these matters. This was a kind of puriazum, because it can be seen as a decision in its own affairs. We know of cases when after one or the other incident has been quieted down, like who did, for example. in the Polish trucking union, previously sanctioned punishments for players were withdrawn or reduced. Therefore, this condition influenced a low assessment of the Polish system of disciplinary case law in matters of doping. The project prepared by the Ministry of Sport enables services such as the Police, Customs Service to be more effective in detecting and prosecuting the smuggling and trafficking of doping substances prohibited in sport, and in particular steroids, which are increasingly interested in organised crime groups as an alternative to drug trafficking. Such records certainly meet today's challenges in the fight for pure sport of professionals and amateurs. The legal situation of the agency's anti-doping controllers has also been clearly and concretely identified. They shall be protected in the performance of their duties or in connection with their duties and shall be subject to the criminal liability provided for by the Penal Code for public officials. The bill is, for the time being, a very innovative, modern document when it comes to solving the problem of combating doping in sport, in legislation, in state law. I am convinced that it will be a cure for activities that have nothing to do with sport today. Players who engage in unauthorised methods to improve their fitness and thus achieve sporting results at the cost of health, honor, disillusionment of fans and discredit of sports authorities, as well as state authorities, must consider it to be treated and judged as a normal common crime.
