ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u286	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for the positive position and recommendations of all clubs regarding the government's anti-doping law in sport. This project is very expected in the sport environment, which not only improves the efficiency of the fight against doping in our country, but also strengthens the image of Poland as a country leading a determined policy for honesty in such an extremely important sphere of public life as sport. I would just like to remind you that the fundamental principles of this project concern the establishment of a Polish Anti-Doping Agency - strong, equipped with broad competences and appropriate financial and human resources - and also the creation of legal conditions for cooperation, including investigative, agencies with services such as the Police or Customs and Treasury, and finally to allow the prosecution by the relevant doping services to prosecute the most dangerous substances banned or traded in our country. This law also serves to professionalise anti-doping control activities, firstly by setting high standards for training and testing of controllers, secondly, by assigning them legal protection and the responsibilities of public officials. It is also important to sanction the new entity structure in the law on disciplinary matters concerning doping in sport, where the Polish sports unions are replaced by the Disciplinar Panel operating at the agency. These solutions represent a huge step towards building a strong and reliable anti-doping system that is recognizable worldwide. I would also like to inform the High House that the draft law on combating doping in sport has been notified to the European Commission under number 2017/6/PL. Penal provisions, provisions concerning the processing of personal data during the anti-doping control and the definition of doping in sport are covered by the notification. Mandatory period of suspension of the legislative procedure, so-called Standstill, for this notification, expired on 6 April 2017. During this period no comments or specific opinions were submitted to the project. With regard to the questions of the ladies and gentlemen of the House. So briefly, in terms of Katowice's chances of organizing, hosting the World Anti-Doping Agency Congress in 2019, the chances are very high. Our opponents are Geneva and Maskat, the capital of Oman. We believe deeply, at least we will do everything we can to get this convention to take place in Katowice. We're gonna try, and I'm positive, and I believe that's what's gonna happen, that the odds are pretty good. As far as expenditure is concerned, this limit is due to legal consequences and we have often stressed that we will try this expenditure on the fight against doping, also on the functioning of the Polish Anti-Doping Agency, to strengthen. This also serves to change the legal formula. Let me remind you that POLADA will have the status of a state legal person, i.e. it will also be able to raise external resources, even from UNESCO funds, from WADA funds, from EU funds. We therefore firmly believe that the Agency's budget will continue to grow. What it will be will also depend on the efficiency of functioning of the agency, especially as it will be able to provide services to entities that organize various sports events in Poland. With regard to the financial consequences of players caught in cheering measures, this change and this kind of direction, i.e. enforcement from players of financial claims for preparation, does not require statutory changes. We want to make such records so that they operate in agreements between sports unions and players, between the ministry and unions, and the unions would have to enforce it from players. I think that, in the case of contracts, we will try, after these legislative arrangements have been introduced, in the context of the next year, to ask sports unions to make such records appear. So the player caught in the doping would be obliged to return certain funds that the union had to pay for its preparation. I think this is the next step that is worth doing, and here we are in agreement. As far as the TEU is concerned, this system works retroactively. Only the agency and only the international sports federation can grant such therapeutic exemptions. These are WADA standards, so there's no possibility of any change here. These are world standards we can't interfere with. As far as educational projects are concerned, they are already functioning. The Commission is undertaking a number of educational activities related to awareness of the effects of doping. We also plan to implement such projects next year, and we also want to do so with sports unions. We have adequate funding for this type of action to take, to educate about the impact of the use of doping measures. Finally, I would like to thank the High House for the very substantive and well-managed work on the government bill on combating doping in sport. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u290	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the Government's draft law amending the act on social security benefits in the event of illness and maternity, print no. 1350. On 28 February 2017, the above draft law entered the Sejm. March 8th this year was addressed to first reading in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. March 23rd this year. The first reading of the draft Print Act No 1350 took place at the meeting of the committee in question. The procedure of the bill at this meeting was interrupted in order to agree on the date of entry into force of the law. The draft was examined at a committee meeting on 5 April 2017 and the committee's report is in print No 1422. High Izbo! The project includes changes to the Social Insurance Money Insurance Act in case of illness and maternity resulting from an analysis of the functioning of existing solutions, as well as comments and postulates of the medical environment and current possibilities of the Social Insurance Institution. I would like to remind you that from 1 January 2016, medical certificates of temporary incapacity for work may be issued in electronic form and in the form of a paper document, except that it will only be possible to issue them in paper form until 31 December 2017. Designed regulations allow the issuing of medical certificates to circumvent barriers related to difficulties in handling and accessing the profile of trusted ePUAP and avoid costs related to the purchase of qualified electronic signatures. A new, simpler way to authenticate electronic medical certificates is introduced in the proposed solutions. The Social Insurance Institution shall make available to doctors free of charge its electronic system, by which it can be confirmed, the correctness of the issuing of the medical certificate. This form will also apply to four documents, which are a medical certificate on incapacity for work issued in electronic form, a statement by doctors of an obligation to comply with the rules on temporary incapacity to work and the obligations arising from the provisions of the Social Insurance Money Insurance Act in the event of sickness, maternity and the provisions of the Personal Data Protection Act, a certificate by the Medical Advocate of the Social Security Office stating that there are no objections to working on a specific post, an application for the grant and payment of benefits in the form of an electronic document. The proposed regulations aim to improve the practice of switching from paper to electronic documents. And it should be noted that the new form of issuing a medical certificate on temporary incapacity to work using the means of confirming the origin and integrity of the data available in the electronic system of the Social Insurance Institution will meet the safety standards that are used for similar services offered by the public administration in Poland. High Izbo! The contested draft law at the meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy was unanimously approved, so the committee asks the High House to adopt it. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u293	Thank you very much. Mrs Bożena Henczyca, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u294	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The Act of 25 June 1999 on social security benefits in the case of sickness and maternity governs the rules for the granting of sickness benefits, the way in which they are calculated and the rules for documenting the right to those benefits. The amendment of this law, which took place in 2015, introduced alongside the existing new form of medical certificate on temporary incapacity for work due to illness, hospital stay or the need to take care of a sick family member providing evidence in determining the right to benefits and their amount. From 1 January 2016, medical certificates on temporary incapacity for work may be issued in electronic form and during a transitional period, i.e. until 31 December 2017, also in their current form, on printed matter. The medical certificate shall bear a qualified electronic signature or a signature confirmed by a trusted ePUAP profile via the IT system of the ZUS. However, a qualified electronic signature is a paid tool. An alternative method of authentication of an electronic medical certificate is the use of a free profile of trusted ePUAP, which is time-consuming and complicated. After analysing how the solutions work, also taking into account the medical environment's demands and the current possibilities of the ZUS, it is proposed to introduce a new, additional and simpler way of authentication electronic medical certificates. As a result of the proposed regulations, the medical certificate will be issued in the form of an electronic document signed using the means of confirming the origin and integrity of the data and available in the electronic system made available free of charge by the Social Security Office. The number of medical certificates issued in electronic form should therefore increase, which is important both from a financial perspective and from the perspective of strengthening the desired attitude among the participants in this process. Designed amendment of the article. 59 (1) (a) 9 The aim of the Act is to enable the medical examiner of the ZUS to issue a certificate which is treated on an equal footing with a certificate stating that there are no contraindications to work on a particular post, in the form of an electronic document signed with a qualified electronic signature, with a signature confirmed by a trusted profile of ePUAP or using the means of confirming the origin and integrity of the data available in the IT system of the ZUS. It should also be noted that the proposed amendments do not block the possibility of implementing the target solution of the Medical Specialist's Charter. The proposed solution to issue a medical certificate on temporary incapacity to work using the method of confirming the origin and integrity of the data available in the IT system of the ZUS will meet the level of safety that is used for similar services offered by the public administration in Poland. Our suggestion was that the Act should enter into force on 1 December 2017, and not, as proposed, on 1 July, so that the ZUS would prepare to implement this amendment. Therefore, the Civic Platform Club recommends adopting amendments to the above Act. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

Systems purchased and operated for 600 million a year do not act or reduce bureaucracy. Currently, an electronic medical certificate may be issued by a doctor in the form of a document bearing a qualified electronic signature or a signature endorsed by a trusted ePUAP profile in accordance with the model established by the Social Security Office. However, a qualified electronic signature is a paid tool, and the cost of purchasing a set of such signatures is additionally charged to the doctor, apart from the fact that the taxpayers' money has already been spent for this purpose. An alternative method of authentication of an electronic medical certificate is the use of a free profile of trusted ePUAP, but in the assessment issuing medical certificates the use of ePUAP profile is time-consuming and the tool is very complex in its operation. The new rules allow the issuing of medical certificates to circumvent barriers related to handling and access to the profile of trusted ePUAP, as well as costs related to the purchase of qualified electronic signatures. For this purpose, a new way of confirming the origin and integrity of data available in the IT system made available free of charge by the Social Security Office will be used. Of course, Kukiz15 will support this law, it will support this amendment, because it can only make life easier for citizens to live. They won't have to go to the doctor or deliver them to their employers for medical certificates. However, using this opportunity, it is worth recalling that so far Polish taxpayers spent nearly PLN 4 billion on IT systems, for the computerization of ZUS. For imaging: 1 hour of maintenance of computers and systems in the ZUS costs 90,000. zł. Also for imaging, to see what the amount is, I calculated what it looks like when it comes to where the money comes from. We have in Poland less than 2 million small companies and single-person activities, which pay a pension fee of PLN 499 per month. If we multiply this contribution, we get about 800 million. It means that single-person companies that pay a contribution to their pensions, actually instead of paying for those pensions, simply keep computers in the ZUS. So I'm going to end with the question: How much is the next revision of the IT systems and whether someone will then be responsible for the wrong and impractical systems bought with money, which could be used for example. for the execution of judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal on the compensation of allowances for persons caring for adults with disabilities who currently receive PLN 520 and should receive PLN 1400? ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u312	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to thank the representatives of all parliamentary clubs for their unanimity, for the unanimous support of the government's draft law amending the Social Insurance Money Insurance Act in the event of illness and maternity. It can be said that the need to amend this law is due to the unsatisfactory pace of implementation of the new form of medical certificates on incapacity for work, namely electronic form. As was mentioned in the discussion, we are in transition. This transitional period started on 1 January 2016. During this transitional period doctors are entitled to issue medical certificates in two forms, i.e. in traditional, paper and electronic form. The current pace of implementation of the electronic form, which, according to current legislation, will be the only form of medical certification from 1 January 2018, is simply unsatisfactory. I would like to confirm the data that were presented today. Less than 3% doctors authorised to issue medical certificates shall use electronic form. Less than 3% of medical certificates on incapacity for work are certificates which are issued in this form. Actually, it's the rate of implementation of the so-called. e-releases, e-releases issued in electronic form, we already regard today as too slow to achieve this goal, which is simply the full implementation of the new electronic form from 1 January 2018. Hence, our concern is to ensure that any possible barriers, including financial, also organisational, related to the implementation of the so-called. remove e-releases. Among the barriers that are pointed out by the environmental stakeholders themselves, i.e. the medical environment, two barriers are mentioned: the financial barrier associated with the use of the so-called. e-signatures and organisational barriers related to the time-consuming use of the trusted ePUAP profile. Hence the need whether we considered it necessary to correct the two existing authentication mechanisms for medical certificates and there is a need to introduce a third mechanism using the Social Insurance Institution's IT system. According to the original wording of the government project, the date of entry into force of the draft law discussed today was scheduled for 1 July 2017 and was maintained during parliamentary work in the Parliamentary Committee. It turned out that the changes necessary for the IT system of the Social Insurance Institution, its adaptation to new functionalities, are time-consuming enough that it is not possible to implement or implement this project by 1 July 2017, hence, during the work of the parliamentary committee, this deadline at the request of the Social Insurance Institution, at the request of the Social Insurance Institution was postponed to 1 December 2017. I would like to address the questions raised during the discussion. With regard to the question of the date of entry into force of the Act, I have the impression that I have explained why the deadline was being postponed. I would also like to draw attention to the fact that the delay to 1 December 2017 entails that this new form of medical sick leave authentication will allow doctors to benefit in the short term, it can be said, before this planned deadline, from which the electronic release will be the only form of issuing such exemptions. Let me remind you, I was talking about this, 1 January 2018, so we are actually dealing with a situation in which one month before the end of this so-called. This transitional period, you can say, new functionality, a new form of authentication of medical certificates will be applicable. In the course of the discussion, the Member representing the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, tabled an amendment which provides for a six-month delay in this transitional period by 1 July 2018 or by 30 June 2018. I would like to say that, from the point of view of this, it is possible to extend this transitional period and to allow doctors to use this new form of authentication of the medical certificates issued, this is a rational solution, but there will, as I understand it, be an opportunity to discuss this issue during the session of the Parliamentary Committee, where this amendment will be discussed. As regards the question of improving the authentication of medical certificates, I would like to say here that this issue has also been discussed with the Minister of Health. It is also a problem of some compatibility of systems or, more specifically, the software used by doctors and software used by the Social Insurance Institution, but already such facilities for improving authentication, issuing medical certificates in electronic form are present in the software used by doctors primarily by primary health care. In fact, this discussion with the Minister of Health showed that we would be able to talk about a certain success when the time needed to issue an electronic medical certificate would be shorter than the time needed to issue a paper medical certificate. Honestly, I personally expressed my opinion that in any case it seems to me that this medical certificate issued in an electronic version simply... This show will take less time. This depends to a large extent on the various types of detailed solutions resulting from this software. I don't want to talk about these details, but we're doing everything we can to say that, as far as the short one is concerned, because, as the Minister said here, less than 30 seconds, the time to issue a medical certificate in a paper version is just to match it. As far as solutions are concerned in other countries, I would like to say that this element was taken into account when discussing the introduction of this solution at all, i.e. the possibility of issuing electronic medical certificates. I would like to remind you that this issue has already been resolved on 15 May 2015. At that time, the Sejm decided that after a two-year transition period this form - so-called. e-releases, i.e. exemptions issued in electronic form, will be the only form of medical certificates in circulation. I would like to say that this is actually the norm for modern social security systems. As regards the questions concerning the transition period and functionality, I feel that I have answered questions about ways to improve this process of issuing medical certificates. On the other hand, with regard to these two alternative methods of issuing medical certificates so far, I can say that, of course, the paper version so-called. Doctor's parole has its drawbacks. This fundamental disadvantage is that it limits the ability of the doctor to check the correctness of the medical certificate. According to the data we received from the Social Insurance Institution, in Poland more than 52% of sick leave is short-term. It can be said that this traditional form - related to the issue of a medical certificate in paper form, then the sending of this certificate to the Social Insurance Institution, the removal of this certificate by the Social Security Office - is in fact impossible to carry out checks on the correctness of the issuing of such certificates, medical exemptions less than 14 days, which is an argument in favour of this process of dissemination of a new electronic form of medical certificates as soon as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u346	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of the Republicans, it is my honor to present our position on the government's draft amendment to the Foreign Service Act. The Act amends or cooperates with provisions aimed at preventing further employment in the foreign service of persons who worked in or with State security authorities in the years 1944 and 1990. We stress clearly that as Republicans, we are strong supporters of the diplomacy of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. In general, the fact that such persons continue to perform their functions in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, and despite the passing of more than a quarter of a century since the liberation of Poland from the eyes of communism, must be assessed clearly critically. How is it possible that until today, in the foreign service of the independent Republic of Poland there is still room for secret co-workers of the Polish People's Republic or graduates of Soviet universities? The loyalty of employees of the Polish diplomatic service to the independent Polish state must be indisputable. Those who served the communist state, which was dependent on Soviet Russia, should in no way be covered by the guarantee of trust. We are therefore pleased with this comprehensive review of the Polish diplomatic staff, which is announced as a result of the introduced rules. We consider it necessary and appropriate to carry out such a review. In foreign service institutions, which have not been reformed for many years, many people are still employed from the so-called. The post-war labor and peasant enrollment. It is these people who have been active in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs for decades on the principle of clanality. These people have not had and have not so far had any qualifications to work in the field of foreign service. This situation applies primarily to foreign institutions and technical units, which have grown to a huge extent. We hope, therefore, that the statutory review of staff will not be carried out at the expense of reliable staff and will at the same time reduce undoubtedly existing pathologies and give an opportunity to those who have the right powers to perform foreign service. We also hope that one of the consequences, as announced, of introducing the changes proposed by the designers will be to increase the recruitment for foreign service through the diplomatic/consulary application. Recruitment to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is the best way to complement staff in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, because it allows young and competent people to be admitted to work, not party cameras. However, we do not like the statutory requirement contained in the draft regulations, which allows managers in the foreign service to be able to document the knowledge of only one foreign language. After all, we must remember that executive positions in foreign service are also higher positions in civil service. These posts should only be reserved for professional diplomats. We therefore call for the repeal of this provision or for it to be subject to a documented knowledge of at least two foreign languages, including necessarily English. We are afraid that without these provisions, this provision may provide a gateway to the fact that executive positions in the foreign service will be taken by people who will not have the necessary qualifications for this. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Circle of Poselski Republicans is in favour of further proceding this law in the relevant committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Bravo.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u392	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour and pleasure to present to you a draft amendment to the law on value added tax - a project that Kukiz has proposed as part of our legislative action on Mondays of freedom. This is an action which is a complete novelty in the Polish Parliament, namely a deregulation action, simplifying the law, proposing the liquidation or merger of unnecessary, overdeveloped state institutions. This project is unique and very simple at the same time. Exceptional in the sense that very rarely the High House has the opportunity to discuss projects that reduce taxes. You usually deal with projects that raise or introduce new taxes and taxes. What's the project? The point is that we want to put an end to a situation that deviates from reality, from technological progress and is contrary to a common sense approach. If you buy a book, e.g. The free choice of Milton and Rose Friedman is charged with 5% VAT. If you would like to buy the same book in electronic form and read e.g. on the tablet, it will be taxed at a rate of 23 percent. We propose to introduce an electronic rate of 5% on books, the same as on paper books, and we also propose to reduce the rate for electronic editions to 8% of the electronic press, just as paper newspapers are taxed. Currently, the share of e-books and e-publications in the reading market is around 3.5%. This low share is, of course, due to the under-taxation of these publications, which in fact constitutes discrimination against electronic publications. We are referring here to the principle of equal taxation. Meaning books and magazines, regardless of their form, should be taxed equally. Why? Because when a reader buys a book, he acquires a certain content, not a medium on which it is written. The observance of the principle of equal VAT therefore obliges the legislator to make equal taxation of the sale of content irrespective of what medium it is written on, whether physical, online or electronic. The project, we hope, will have a positive impact on reading in Poland, which is not well. According to the National Library's research, only 37% in 2015 citizens had one or more books in their hands. As we know, people are more and more eager to use electronic media, so the lower price of e-Publishing, e-books will be positive also for reading. As for the impact on the budget, if we approach it in a static way, the budget would lose about PLN 63 million, while we are convinced, and so point out all the studies that at the time of tax reduction higher sales of e-books, e-publications will also compensate the state budget for the loss of these revenues. The draft was brought before the Legislative Commission on the grounds of allegations of its non-compliance with European Union law. I would therefore like to refer to this because I expect the Civic Platform to raise this issue because it is the only group that has voted against this project so far. Well, ladies and gentlemen, in France and Italy there are lower VAT rates for electronic books and electronic publications - in France this is 5.5% In Italy, 4%. And it turns out you can. I mean, in Brussels, the Commission is working to make it possible to tax lower rates of electronic books and electronic publications. On 1 December last year, such a project was presented, these works are ongoing. According to my information, they are only blocked by the Czech Republic, which here have some political tactics, but sooner or later it will happen. Neither France nor Italy are held liable at this time. Therefore, I would like to encourage you to have an individual, courageous policy towards the European Union as well. On the other hand, the Ministry of Development, the Ministry of Finance, would like to encourage - since Minister Morawiecki often repeats that he is placing new technologies on - to stop discrimination against those who use these new technologies. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u394	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This important law concerns lower VAT on e-books and electronic publications. It can be said that there are at least two reasons to be satisfied with the proposed legislation and one serious problem. I'll start with the reasons why we can be happy. It is also appropriate what concerns the substance of this problem, because the situation in which normal books are simply covered with 5% VAT and 23 percent e-books is at least incomprehensible from the consumer's point of view. The introduction of this bill also has a personal dimension for me, because I was doing business myself, among others. an antique shop and a bookstore, which is why I understand this issue well. Finally, if fraud is carried out somewhere, you can say, you try to use this lower VAT rate for books, e.g. by making DVDs in the form of a several-page booklet, and in the case of e-books it is rather difficult to imagine this kind of application. But I also mentioned the problem at the beginning, and the problem was also referred to by the applicant. This problem is undoubtedly the EU regulation, the EU directive that is in force. The European Union has already started in 2015, and this must also be said, among others. on the initiative of Poland, work on amending this directive so as to allow countries to shape lower VAT into e-books and electronic publications. At this moment, it was also mentioned, the Czechs oppose it. As it seems, this is not about the essence of this problem, but about some game in which the Czechs want something else. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u422	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Act consumes the provisions of the EU directive on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State of the European Union. The aim of this Directive is to build the integrity of the cultural heritage of each of the European Union countries by simply facilitating their investigation of the return of cultural objects illegally removed from their countries. The Restitution of Cultural Goods has not yet been regulated in Poland in a uniform, comprehensive way. For this purpose, e.g. were used. civil law regulations. This law defines the authorities competent for the restitution of cultural objects, their tasks and powers, introduces new categories and concepts such as the national cultural good or public collections into Polish legislation, sets out rules for the procedure for the return from the European Union of Polish national cultural objects and on the return from Poland of cultural objects illegally removed, introduces provisions to improve the procedure for ordering the return of cultural objects, eliminates the existing legal gaps and ambiguities in this matter, introduces legal regulations to safeguard the found cultural good, and introduces criminal rules relating primarily to behaviours consisting in obstructing the recovery of national cultural goods. According to the draft law, the Minister of Culture and National Heritage will be responsible for the restitution of cultural goods. He has among others. take action at home and abroad to recover the lost cultural object. The Act also sets out cases where the Minister has an obligation of office to deal with the matter of return of property. It will also be obliged to cooperate with the relevant institutions of the EU countries and to promote mutual consultation on the return of cultural objects within the European Union. To put it simply, it is a project that gives the opportunity to recover illegally lost cultural goods more effectively and more quickly. The Law and Justice Club supports the bill. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u423	Thank you very much. Ms Anna Wasilewska, Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u424	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club vis-à-vis the government's draft law on the restitution of national cultural goods. The project is designed to recover cultural goods that have been unlawfully lost more effectively and quickly. It is also intended to increase the legal security of goods in the event of theft or misappropriation. The project implements in Polish legislation a European Union directive on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a Member State of the European Union. The aim of the Directive is to protect the integrity of the cultural heritage of each of the European Union countries by facilitating their investigation into the return of cultural goods illegally removed from those countries. The Minister of Culture and National Heritage will be responsible for the restitution of cultural goods. The draft law sets out the rules of procedure for the return from the European Union of Polish national cultural goods removed from the country in violation of the law and for the return by Poland of cultural goods unlawfully removed from the European Union in breach of the law, from countries other than the EU. In addition, it is proposed to introduce provisions to improve the procedure for ordering the return of cultural goods, for the safety of the cultural goods found, to extend the obligation to obtain export licences for non-historical cultural goods, to lay down general rules for dealing with cultural goods brought out of Poland in breach of the law after their return. Criminal rules will also be introduced on behaviour that hinders proceedings for the return of the foreign national cultural object. In such a situation, the perpetrator will be sentenced to imprisonment from 3 months to 5 years. In addition, the court will be able to refer to the stated social objective related to the care of monuments. For the Republic of Poland, the importance of restitution of cultural goods and the protection of preserved cultural and national heritage is particularly important. Attention should be paid to the loss of most of the historical resources of this heritage not only in the wake of World War II, but also in previous historical events and circumstances accompanied by the robbery and destruction of cultural goods. While public international law has shaped the principles of interstate restitution, Polish law currently does not contain rules governing restitution cases and civil law issues arising from them. These circumstances justify the need to create regulations in Polish law specifically to ensure effective protection of preserved cultural and national heritage, and therefore the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will be in favour of the law on the restitution of national cultural goods. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u433	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I ask Mr Dariusz Piontkowski. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u434	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the Act on the Educational Information System is mainly related to technological issues and the functioning of the Educational Information System. The SIO architecture changes mainly, improves user communication and modifies the data transfer scheme. The current architecture of the educational information system includes a central database, whose administrator is the Minister for Education and Education, and local databases. These local databases are as many as tens of thousands, which generates a lot of problems, among others. from time to time, there is a problem of losing part of the data. For example, in 2015, the Ministry of Education received more than 4.5 thousand. requests for the possibility of recovering these lost data, which involves a complicated procedure. Today, the creation of a single central database, to which individual individuals will enter data via the internet application, will facilitate the security of the entire system and avoid situations where individual individuals had to recover data. The proposed changes also depart from the sequential input procedure. This will enable the user to enter simultaneously the identification data of teachers or students and field data, i.e. data on information about these students or teachers. To date, if there was an error in the PESEL system, data could not be entered further. This data had to be verified with the PESEL identification system, which sometimes lasted several months. At that time, a school or educational institution had some problems. At the same time, the verification of PESEL data is facilitated. This can also be done by the director, school head or educational institution, and during the transition period it will be possible to temporarily register this student or teacher in the educational information system, which will facilitate the functioning of the entire system. The draft amendment also provides for the simplification of the procedure for access to the database by authorised persons. An electronic authorization is all it takes. Such a person will, of course, receive a login and password. Until now, it was the head of the local government unit, and now he can pass this information on to the head of the educational unit, the school, and the head of the school can pass it on to the authorised employee who will carry out these activities on his behalf. This change in the structure of the educational information system will allow, among others. the ability of several people to enter data in several computer stations in a given unit, which has so far been impossible, and there are quite a number of schools where these data were introduced very much. This will therefore facilitate the introduction of field data into the central system. Of course, the data will be collected by qualified entities who have entered this data within the scope of their authorisation, i.e. the head or employee of the educational unit concerned in respect of those data which he transmitted from the premises of his school. In addition to changes in the system of functioning and construction of the educational information system, there are some simplifications. Among other things, the collection of certain individual data on pedagogical and psychological assistance and family upbringing classes for aggregated data is abandoned. It is similar with material aid of a social nature. It is also a simplification of the functioning of the register of schools and educational establishments. Among other things, the school head may modify the telephone number, fax number, e-mail address, no longer required by the local government unit. It is similar with information about school circuits. So far, the whole area of the school's circuit had to be entered. Now, only information will appear as to whether the school is a secondary school and whether the pupils fulfil the school's obligation in a given secondary school. Another element is the resignation of information covering the student's place of residence in relation to the student's teaching in special schools organised in medicinal subjects. The deadline for the transmission of field data on the remuneration components of teachers is amended. So far, these changes had to be made within 7 days of the change, and now it will only be twice a year. In addition, a number of clarifications and details are introduced in several laws: the Law of Education, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Introductory rules and on the income of local government units, but these are provisions not fundamentally amending those laws, but merely clarifying and facilitating the use of the educational information system. The Law and Justice Club will therefore recommend the adoption of this law without amendment. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]

Art. 4 of the Law on the Publication of Normative Acts states that these reasons must be included in the State's important interest clause. There is no information in this regard and therefore there is a risk that some of the provisions of the project may be declared incompatible with the Constitution. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u437	Thank you very much. Mr Józef Brynkus, Kukiz,15 has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u438	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the Act amending the Act on the Education Information System, contained in print No 1434. The educational information system is used to obtain information that is important for the conduct of State education policy at every administrative level, to determine the effectiveness of the use of public funds for the financing of educational tasks and to improve the quality of education. In practice, it was used as a source of data for sharing educational subsidies. Currently, the education information system is a complex IT tool that supports a large number of different reporting units and also collects a huge amount of data. The educational information system is a tool cooperating with two large and complex state systems, i.e. PESEL and REGON. The draft law significantly modifies the functioning of the educational information system towards its improvement and simplification. This is also the result of catching up after the previous power, which unfortunately poses a certain threat to the implementation of the project - an organisational and financial threat. The essence of the changes are technological modifications, hence the need to consult a specific version with the Ministry of Digitalization. The most important changes are the dismantling of local databases, currently administered by the heads of transferors to the central database of the educational information system, i.e. above all, public directors, non-public schools and educational institutions. They are replaced by the database of the educational information system, hereinafter referred to as the SIO database, and the ICT system. Another change is the revision of the verification rules in the PESEL system of identification data, which is intended to facilitate correction of irregularities and thus reduce the number of unverified pupils and teachers. The procedure for granting authorisations to access the database is also changing as a result of the contested law. Another amendment concerns the abandonment of the transmission of individual data in favour of aggregated data on psychological and pedagogical assistance provided to pupils, participation in family education activities and the provision of social material assistance by municipalities. They will be transmitted as aggregate data. Very important, but also easier to operate entities are solutions for modification and updating of data in the Register of Schools and Educational Institutions and information about school circuits. It is also important to change the possibility of obtaining information from the system relating to pupils. They may be used by the courts and bodies of the State carrying out public tasks. It seems like you have to believe. introducing a draft law that, with the new technological solution, the functioning of the educational information system, hardware requirements will be much lower than today, which will also significantly reduce the costs of the system. Changes are necessary, even though there is no basic data available in the current system, as highlighted by the Supreme Audit Chamber, and the security of the so-called. sensitive data relating to pupils and teachers, even though the system was implemented with considerable financial effort. The Kukiz Poselski Club15 will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u452	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! This year is the 115th anniversary of the birth of Władysław Sebyla, a great poet murdered in Starobielsk. These April days are just passing another anniversary of the Katyn crime. So I pay a deep tribute to the memory of Polish officers - the elite of the Polish people, who by order of Stalin were murdered by the NKVD with a shot to the back of the head. They were buried in mass graves. They were condemned not only to a terrible biological death, but also to wipe them out of national consciousness. One of them was the poet Władysław Sebyła. Władysław Sebyła was born on 6 February 1902 in Kłobuck near Częstochowa. When he's only three, his mother dies. My father and his sons, Władysław and Stefan, are moving to Bedzin, where he receives a teaching position. Władysław finishes primary school in Będzin, he attends middle school in Sosnowiec, where he also gives his graduation. Participates in the Silesian Uprisings and the Polish-Bolshevik War in 1920. In 1921 he is going to Warsaw for studies. After a year of studying at the University of Warsaw, he moves to the University of Warsaw, where he finishes Polish studies. Sebyła is an artistic omnibus, talented poet, painter and musician. He wrote with great responsibility for the word. He is the author of three volumes of poetry: Rat Catcher Songs, Ethical Concerto and Thought Images. Since 1927 he has been associated with the poetry group Kwadryga, to which he belonged among others. Gałczyński, becoming her soul. Soon he became the editor-in-chief of the magazine Quadriga, raising his level significantly. He prints, reviews and writes poems, directs the poetic criticism department in Polish Radio. In 1938. The Polish Academy of Literature awarded Sebyle the highest award for her entire work - Złoty Wawrzyn. Władysław Sebyła also starts studying art history. He meets Karol Szymanowski and is a frequent guest in Atma. He even started composing himself, unfortunately, his songs were missing. Leon Pomirowski, an excellent critic and researcher of contemporary literature in the 1930s, says the work of Sebła: The poet has an intellectual attitude towards life, he can reach people. A poem free, fluent, figuratively testify to a vivid imagination. He is a follower of the most fertile in thought, aesthetically and morally threads of Polish poetry. And so about Władysław Sebyle Tadeusz Januszewski, under a significant title of "Who Burned You, My Comrades," paraphrasing one of the poet's visionary poems: Sebła focuses on poetry on worldview and moral issues, she conducts a discourse with herself, whose purpose is to ask fundamental questions more than to find answers. These seekers give direction to the eternal question about the existence of God, about the moral order of the world, about the place of man in this world, about the rank of human dignity. For his philosophy, in which metaphysics intertwined peculiarly with reality, Sebyla developed his own poetry style. His relationships with medieval imagination, poetry of romance and modernism were seen. Władysława Sebyla met the death of the worst soldier that could happen - hands tied with barbed wire and shot in the back of the head. For the writer, however, there is a crueler death - condemning him to silence and silence around him. And that's what he writes about Vladislav Sebyle. Przełat Peszkowski, prisoner of Kozielska, chaplain of the Katyn Family: For years you were unfashionable in Poland because why did you die in Kharkov? And now to read yourself properly into you, to want to understand you, you can sit down with a volume of your poems and freely keep, search out the secrets of your feelings, thoughts, ups, perspectives, sounds and images from the verses. You could look at the world, take it in your own way, and pass it on to your descendants, your readers. It feels like reading your poems. I thank you for your thoughts, for your sharpness like a surgeon's reflections that cut out something bad, low in the nation, some kind of poverty. And I thank you for that. And even though for years your voice in Poland in an inhuman way, cruel was jammed because you died badly in the Polish People's Republic, but now it reaches us with a multiple, reflective echo. Yes, you've had many gifts from God. This means that you constantly surprise us with the form, the image, the painted words, the image from which the sounds come out. I am incredibly grateful to you not only because you gave your country your life, but because you have enriched our homeland. I believe we all feel the same way. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u457	Thank you very much. Mr Sylwester Khrushch, Kukiz,15 has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u458	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We just had two Silesian, Upper Silesian statements. Mine will also be like this, because today we celebrate the 144th anniversary of Wojciech Korfante's birth - the Silesian politician and leader of the Silesian uprisings. In the Second Republic of Poland he was one of the leaders of Christian Democracy and one of the most important figures of the fight for the connection between Silesia and Poland. Between 1901 and 1908, he was a member of the National League in which he worked with Roman Dmowski. From the very beginning he fought for the Polish case, first as a Member of the Reichstag, where he made a request to join the Polish state of all Polish lands taken after the Prussian elections. After World War I he was part of the General Council of the People's Council in Poznań, which was the Wielkopolska government during the Wielkopolska Uprising. Since 1901, he has worked as editor-in-chief of Górnoślązaka, a newspaper in Upper Silesia. In 1902, he was sent to prison in Wronki for publishing articles to the Germans and to my brothers Upper Silesia. In the years 1903-1912 and 1918 he was a Member of the Reichstag and in the years 1903-1918 to the Prussian Landtag, where he represented the Polish Circle. In the reborn Poland in the years 1922-1930 he was a Member of the Sejm for Christian Democracy. In the face of the failure of the mission to create a government by Artur Śliwiński, Korfanta was designated by the Main Commission of the Polish Sejm as Prime Minister. However, against the protest of his opponent, the head of state of Józef Piłsudski, and the threat of a general strike by the PPS did not start the formation of the government, and the committee withdrew his nomination. From October to December 1923, he was Deputy Prime Minister of the Government of Vincent Witos and his advisor. In the spring of 1935, in fear of repression, he went to emigration to Czech Prague. In April 1939, after the Third Reich's declaration of non-aggression and non-violence, he returned to Poland, where he was arrested and imprisoned in Pawiak. Despite public protests, he spent three months there. He was released on July 20 because of a severe illness. He died in Warsaw... died in Katowice, sorry, August 17th. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u459	Thank you very much. Mr Janusz Sanocki, not associated, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-20-sejm-40-1.u460	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On 26 March in Warsaw, representatives of social justice repair committees met and set up an open civic social movement aimed at repairing justice and exerting civic pressure on power, on the Sejm, on parties, on the whole system - in order to remove the extraordinary pathology that is spreading in the Polish judiciary, in the prosecutor's office, throughout the system of justice. Citizens are harmed by unlawful judgments, by the arrogance of the courts, by long-term trials, and these injuries do not find any compensation. Therefore, there must be thorough, deep reforms, deeper and further along the lines of those proposed at the moment by Minister Ziobro or the Law and Justice, moving further towards the establishment of external control of the justice system, over the judiciary, because in the construction of the Polish courts the article was violated. 4 Constitution - the fundamental principle of national sovereignty, the sovereignty of the nation over every authority. And that is the main reason, that this control does not exist, social, external, that judges control themselves, choose themselves, judge themselves, that is the main reason for the evil and corruption of the Polish judiciary. Therefore, our goal, all these civic committees that arise, is not only to document the harm that citizens have suffered from the courts - we also promote a solution that will restore, will establish a social control in the form of the election of judges, in the form of jury cases, in the form of judges of peace at this lowest level. There should be reforms in this direction, there should be systemic changes in the Law on the System of General Courts, the Act on the National Judicial Council - and the change of the whole system. Of course, there should also be the choice of prosecutors. The profession of a judge - this is not essentially a profession, but a public authority - should be preceded by a long-term trial period. It can't be that young people without experience, without some tradition, who are not tested by anyone, but after a high school court in Cracow. This is a false, wrong path of access to the profession, which just makes us in the courtroom to an inexperienced judge who simply treats the parties, the victims, at times unable to make the right decision. The judges must meet the criterion of social support, i.e. they must be judged by society either in indirect or direct elections - that is to be considered. We propose the establishment of such voivodship judicial councils, which would come from general elections and which would only elect judges, judge them afterwards and act as judicial reviewers. So that these are proposals for the Social Recovery Movement of the Justice and State System - such a name, because we are talking about the fact that social control over judicial authority should be established, but also the work of the Sejm should be changed. We call for the wronged citizens to be heard by the justice committee. We also see the need to change citizens' access to control of Members so that the Sejm can work smoothly. It is also necessary to change the electoral ordination and introduce one-mandate electoral districts in order to restore full rights to citizens in the electoral process. Thank you.
